# CCEA GCE Mathematics Specification Map
**Source 1:** Specification(19).pdf  
**Source 2:** Elaboration Document(19).pdf  
**Date generated:** 2026-05-22  
**Purpose:** Master curriculum map for CCEA AS/A Level Mathematics revision project.

---
## 1. How to Use This Document
This document is organised as a specification-first curriculum map. The official CCEA Specification supplies the unit structure and learning outcomes. The Elaboration Document supplies the extra guidance, examples and boundaries where they are available. Use it as a searchable command deck for building lesson notes, question packs and revision trackers.

For lesson planning, start with the unit overview, then use each topic summary and expanded learning outcome notes to decide what a lesson must cover. For question-bank tagging, use the LO IDs and suggested tags so that every question can be linked to a precise specification target. For revision tracking, copy the tracker template in Section 10 and mark each LO as Not started, In progress, Complete or Needs review. For identifying syllabus gaps, scan the Master Topic Index and the learning outcome tables for missing notes, missing question packs or missing diagrams. For diagrams, widgets and interactive resources, use the visual/widget column as a planning prompt only, not as a requirement to build every possible resource.

## 4. Overarching Themes
### Mathematical language and notation
**Official specification idea:** Use mathematical language and syntax correctly, including equality, implication, equivalence, necessary and sufficient conditions, set notation and function language.

**Student-friendly meaning:** Students must write mathematics in a way that means exactly what it says. Symbols are not decoration; they are grammar.

**Where it commonly appears:** Every proof, algebra step, function question, probability calculation and statistical interpretation.

**Project tags:** `#MathematicalLanguage`, `#Notation`, `#Communication`

### Proof and reasoning
**Official specification idea:** Use proof by deduction and exhaustion, disproof by counterexample, and at A level proof by contradiction. Critique arguments and justify methods.

**Student-friendly meaning:** Students should be able to build a mathematical argument rather than only carry out a procedure.

**Where it commonly appears:** Identities, series formulae, trig proofs, numerical-method reasoning, modelling critiques.

**Project tags:** `#Proof`, `#Reasoning`, `#Justification`

### Modelling and problem solving
**Official specification idea:** Recognise structure, simplify and abstract, translate contexts into models, use models, interpret outputs, refine assumptions and evaluate appropriateness.

**Student-friendly meaning:** Students should move between the real problem and the mathematical version without losing the meaning on the journey.

**Where it commonly appears:** Mechanics, statistics, exponentials, functions, parametric equations, differential equations, numerical methods.

**Project tags:** `#Modelling`, `#ProblemSolving`, `#InterpretContext`

### Diagrams, graphs and interpretation
**Official specification idea:** Extract information from diagrams, construct diagrams, sketch graphs and use diagrams to solve problems, including in mechanics.

**Student-friendly meaning:** A good diagram can be the difference between fog and a lighthouse.

**Where it commonly appears:** Coordinate geometry, transformations, trig graphs, calculus, forces, projectiles, probability and statistics.

**Project tags:** `#Diagram`, `#SketchGraph`, `#InterpretGraph`

### Technology, calculators, graphing tools and spreadsheets
**Official specification idea:** Technology, especially mathematical/statistical graphing tools and spreadsheets, must permeate teaching. Calculators must include an iterative function and distribution/statistics functionality. Computer algebra systems are not allowed in examinations.

**Student-friendly meaning:** Use technology to explore, check and visualise, but do not rely on CAS-style symbolic solving in exams.

**Where it commonly appears:** Numerical methods, graph transformations, statistics, distributions, large data set work and modelling.

**Project tags:** `#UseCalculator`, `#UseSpreadsheet`, `#GraphingTool`

### Communication of solutions
**Official specification idea:** Construct extended arguments, communicate solutions in context and use correct notation.

**Student-friendly meaning:** The answer is not complete until the reasoning and interpretation can be understood by someone else.

**Where it commonly appears:** All units, especially multi-step pure problems, mechanics modelling and statistical interpretation.

**Project tags:** `#Communication`, `#ExamTechnique`, `#WorkedSolution`

### Evaluation of models and limitations
**Official specification idea:** Evaluate accuracy, limitations, assumptions and possible refinements of mathematical models and numerical solutions.

**Student-friendly meaning:** Students should ask whether a mathematical answer is sensible, not just whether it is calculationally tidy.

**Where it commonly appears:** Exponential models, mechanics assumptions, probability modelling, differential equations and numerical methods.

**Project tags:** `#EvaluateModel`, `#Limitations`, `#Assumptions`

## 6. Unit-by-Unit Specification Map
Each unit below contains a unit overview, a topic summary, a learning outcomes table and expanded notes for every official learning outcome. LO IDs are stable project identifiers and can be used in lesson notes, question banks and revision trackers.

---
# AS1: Pure Mathematics
## Unit Overview
**Role in course:** Compulsory pure unit for both AS and A level. It supplies the algebra, graphs, trigonometry, calculus and vector foundations needed across the rest of the course.

**Assessment details:** External written examination, 1 hour 45 mins; 100 raw marks if stated; 60% of AS; 24% of A level.

**Broad content areas:** Algebra and functions, Co-ordinate geometry in the x,y plane, Sequences and series, Trigonometry, Exponentials and logarithms, Differentiation, Integration, Vectors.

**Important student-facing comments:** Keep the official learning outcomes as the boundary. Use elaboration guidance to clarify examples and limits. Treat diagrams, calculators, spreadsheets and graphing tools as planning supports, not as extra syllabus content.

**Links to overarching themes:** mathematical language, modelling, graph/diagram interpretation, calculator/spreadsheet use, communication and evaluation of assumptions.

## Topic: Algebra and functions
### Topic Summary
The algebra toolkit for AS Pure: indices, surds, quadratics, equations, inequalities, polynomials and the first layer of function-graph thinking. It is the course's algebraic engine room: almost every later pure topic borrows from it.

### Expanded Learning Outcome Notes
#### AS1-AF-LO001: The laws of indices for all rational exponents

**Official specification wording:**  
demonstrate understanding of and use the laws of indices for all rational exponents

**Elaboration document guidance:**  
No additional elaboration guidance found in the supplied Elaboration Document.

**What this means in plain English:**  
Know how to use the laws of indices for all rational exponents

**What students should be able to do:**
- choose the appropriate trigonometric relation
- work in the required angle measure and interval
- use graphs, identities or formulae to justify the result

**Required depth and boundaries:**  
Use the official wording as the ceiling. No extra limits were found in the supplied Elaboration Document, so avoid adding more advanced methods as if they were required.

**Useful prerequisites:**  
Higher Tier GCSE algebra, powers, roots, factorising, solving equations, graph sketching.

**Typical exam/question-bank tags:**  
`#AS1`, `#AlgebraFunctions`, `#SolveEquation`, `#SketchGraph`

**Possible visuals or interactive supports:**  
None needed; short rule table may help.

**Links to other learning outcomes:**  
AS1-AF-LO002

**Source reference:**  
Specification: p. 9-10  
Elaboration Document: p. 4-5

#### AS1-AF-LO007: Simultaneous equations in two variables by elimination and by substit...

**Official specification wording:**

#### AS1-AF-LO009: Linear and quadratic inequalities in a single variable and such inequ...

**Official specification wording:**

**Elaboration document guidance:**  
Includes inequalities reducible to linear and quadratic inequalities, for example $\frac{a}{x}<b$ implies $ax<bx^2$.

**What this means in plain English:**  
Solve linear and quadratic inequalities in a single variable and interpret such inequalities graphically, including inequalities with brackets and fractions

**What students should be able to do:**
- recognise the key features of the graph or diagram
- sketch or interpret it using correct scale and notation
- connect graphical features to equations or contexts

**Required depth and boundaries:**  
Include linear/quadratic inequalities and those reducible to them. Take care with domains and sign changes in fractional inequalities; do not extend into general rational inequality theory beyond the specified forms.

**Useful prerequisites:**  
Higher Tier GCSE algebra, powers, roots, factorising, solving equations, graph sketching.

#### AS1-AF-LO016: Simple graph transformations

**Official specification wording:**  
demonstrate understanding of the effect of simple transformations on the graph of $y=f(x)$, including sketching associated graphs: $y=af(x)$, $y=f(x)+a$, $y=f(x+a)$ and $y=f(ax)$

**Elaboration document guidance:**  
The Elaboration Document lists the simple transformation forms $y=af(x)$, $y=f(x+a)$ and $y=f(ax)$. The Specification page was visually inspected and includes $y=f(x)+a$ as well.

**What this means in plain English:**  
Understand the effect of simple transformations on the graph of $y=f(x)$, including sketching associated graphs: $y=af(x)$, $y=f(x)+a$, $y=f(x+a)$ and $y=f(ax)$

**What students should be able to do:**
- recognise the key features of the graph or diagram
- sketch or interpret it using correct scale and notation
- connect graphical features to equations or contexts

**Required depth and boundaries:**  
Use the Specification as authority: vertical stretch, vertical translation, horizontal translation and horizontal stretch/compression are included. Do not treat more complex compositions as AS1-only requirements unless the question supplies them.

**Useful prerequisites:**  
Higher Tier GCSE algebra, powers, roots, factorising, solving equations, graph sketching.

**Typical exam/question-bank tags:**  
`#AS1`, `#AlgebraFunctions`, `#SolveEquation`, `#SketchGraph`

**Possible visuals or interactive supports:**  
Desmos or HTML/JS slider widget for transformations; graph sketch.

**Links to other learning outcomes:**  
AS1-AF-LO015, AS1-CG-LO001, AS1-CG-LO002

**Source reference:**  
Specification: p. 9-10  
Elaboration Document: p. 4-5

## Topic: Co-ordinate geometry in the $x,y$ plane
### Topic Summary
Straight lines and circles in coordinate form: gradients, midpoints, distances, circle equations and tangents. This is geometry with an algebraic lantern.

## Topic: Sequences and series
### Topic Summary
AS1 introduces the finite binomial expansion for positive integer powers and the notation that supports it. It is a compact topic but it later blossoms into A2 series work.

### Learning Outcomes Table
| LO ID | Specification learning outcome | Elaboration guidance | Student-friendly meaning | Required depth / boundaries | Suggested lesson tags | Suggested question-bank tags | Visual / widget opportunities | Source pages |

## Topic: Trigonometry

#### AS1-TRIG-LO007: Simple trigonometric equations in a given interval, quadratic equatio...

**Official specification wording:**  
solve simple trigonometric equations in a given interval, including quadratic equations in $\sin$, $\cos$ and $\tan$ and equations involving multiples of the unknown angle

**Elaboration document guidance:**  
Examples include solving $\cos(2x-60^\circ)=0.5$ for $0^\circ<x<360^\circ$, $1+\sin(2x)=0.5$ for $-180^\circ<x<180^\circ$, $2\sin^2x-\cos x=1$ for $0^\circ\leq x\leq360^\circ$, and $\tan(3x)=2$ for $0^\circ\leq x\leq180^\circ$.

**What this means in plain English:**  
Solve simple trigonometric equations in a given interval, including quadratic equations in $\sin$, $\cos$ and $\tan$ and equations involving multiples of the unknown a...

**What students should be able to do:**
- choose the appropriate trigonometric relation
- work in the required angle measure and interval
- use graphs, identities or formulae to justify the result

**Required depth and boundaries:**  
Stay with simple trigonometric equations in stated intervals, including quadratics in one trig function and multiples of the unknown angle. General inverse-trig domain/range theory is A2.

**Useful prerequisites:**  
GCSE trigonometry, angles, exact values where known, solving equations, graph sketching.

**Typical exam/question-bank tags:**  
`#AS1`, `#Trigonometry`, `#SolveTrigEquation`, `#TrigIdentity`

**Possible visuals or interactive supports:**  
Graph sketch; solution interval number line; Desmos graph intersections.

**Links to other learning outcomes:**  
AS1-TRIG-LO006

Specification: p. 11  
Elaboration Document: p. 7

## Topic: Exponentials and logarithms
### Topic Summary
Exponential and logarithmic functions, their inverse relationship, logarithm laws and basic growth/decay modelling. This topic is the bridge between algebraic equations and real-world continuous change.

demonstrate understanding of and use $\ln x$ as the inverse function of $e^x$

## Topic: Differentiation
### Topic Summary
AS differentiation treats derivatives as gradients and rates of change, then uses them to find tangents, normals, stationary points and increasing/decreasing behaviour.

## Topic: Integration
### Topic Summary
AS integration is reverse differentiation and area under curves. Students learn indefinite and definite integration for powers of $x$ and use it to calculate simple areas.

### Learning Outcomes Table
| LO ID | Specification learning outcome | Elaboration guidance | Student-friendly meaning | Required depth / boundaries | Suggested lesson tags | Suggested question-bank tags | Visual / widget opportunities | Source pages |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AS1-INT-LO001 | demonstrate understanding of and use indefinite integration as the reverse of differentiation | No additional elaboration guidance found in the supplied Elaboration Document. | Know how to use indefinite integration as the reverse of differentiation | Use the official wording as the ceiling. No extra limits were found in the supplied Elaboration Document, so avoid adding more advanced methods as if they were required. | `#AS1`, `#Integration` | `#AS1`, `#Integrate`, `#AreaUnderCurve` | Derivative/integral inverse flowchart. | Spec p. 13; Elaboration p. 10 |
| AS1-INT-LO002 | integrate $x^n$ excluding $n=-1$, and related sums, differences and constant multiples | Includes rational values of $n$. Students should be able to find $f(x)$ given $f'(x)$. | Integrate $x^n$ excluding $n=-1$, and related sums, differences and constant multiples | Use the power rule for $n\ne -1$, including rational powers. The $\int 1/x\,dx$ result is not AS1 here and appears at A2. | `#AS1`, `#Integration` | `#AS1`, `#Integrate`, `#AreaUnderCurve` | Rule table; reverse differentiation checker. | Spec p. 13; Elaboration p. 10 |
| AS1-INT-LO003 | evaluate definite integrals | Includes rational values of $n$. Students should be able to find $f(x)$ given $f'(x)$. | Evaluate definite integrals | Use the official wording as the ceiling and apply the stated elaboration guidance. Do not extend the outcome into methods, functions or contexts not listed unless a question provides them. | `#AS1`, `#Integration` | `#AS1`, `#Integrate`, `#AreaUnderCurve` | Integral evaluation layout; calculator check. | Spec p. 13; Elaboration p. 10 |
| AS1-INT-LO004 | use a definite integral to find the area defined by a curve and either axis | Includes, for example, the area bounded by $y=x(x-2)$ and the lines $x=-1$ and $x=1$. Includes use of definite integration together with areas of trapeziums and triangles, for example finding the area bounded by $y=x^2$ and the line $y=3x$. | Use a definite integral to find the area defined by a curve and either axis | Use the official wording as the ceiling and apply the stated elaboration guidance. Do not extend the outcome into methods, functions or contexts not listed unless a question provides them. | `#AS1`, `#Integration` | `#AS1`, `#Integrate`, `#AreaUnderCurve` | Shaded area graph; coordinate diagram; Desmos area visual. | Spec p. 13; Elaboration p. 10 |

#### AS1-INT-LO004: A definite integral to the area defined by a curve and either axis

**Official specification wording:**  
use a definite integral to find the area defined by a curve and either axis

**Elaboration document guidance:**  
Includes, for example, the area bounded by $y=x(x-2)$ and the lines $x=-1$ and $x=1$. Includes use of definite integration together with areas of trapeziums and triangles, for example finding the area bounded by $y=x^2$ and the line $y=3x$.

**What this means in plain English:**  
Use a definite integral to find the area defined by a curve and either axis

**What students should be able to do:**
- recognise when this idea is relevant
- apply the required method accurately
- communicate the result using correct notation

**Required depth and boundaries:**  
Use the official wording as the ceiling and apply the stated elaboration guidance. Do not extend the outcome into methods, functions or contexts not listed unless a question provides them.

**Useful prerequisites:**  
Differentiation of powers, algebraic expansion, solving equations, area formulae.

**Typical exam/question-bank tags:**  
`#AS1`, `#Integrate`, `#AreaUnderCurve`

**Possible visuals or interactive supports:**  
Shaded area graph; coordinate diagram; Desmos area visual.

**Links to other learning outcomes:**  
AS1-INT-LO003

**Source reference:**  
Specification: p. 13  
Elaboration Document: p. 10

# AS2: Applied Mathematics
## Unit Overview
**Role in course:** Compulsory applied unit for both AS and A level. It assumes AS1 and is split evenly between mechanics and statistics, with modelling and interpretation threaded through both halves.

**Assessment details:** External written examination, 1 hour 15 mins; 70 raw marks if stated; 40% of AS; 16% of A level.

**Broad content areas:** Quantities and units in mechanics, Kinematics, Forces and Newton's laws, Statistical sampling, Data presentation and interpretation, Probability, Statistical distributions.

**Important student-facing comments:** Keep the official learning outcomes as the boundary. Use elaboration guidance to clarify examples and limits. Treat diagrams, calculators, spreadsheets and graphing tools as planning supports, not as extra syllabus content.

**Links to overarching themes:** mathematical language, modelling, graph/diagram interpretation, calculator/spreadsheet use, communication and evaluation of assumptions.

## Topic: Quantities and units in mechanics
### Topic Summary
The language of measurement in mechanics: fundamental SI quantities and the derived quantities used in motion and force problems.

No extra limits were found in the supplied Elaboration Document, so avoid adding more advanced methods as if they were required. | `#AS2`, `#Mechanics`, `#Units` | `#AS2`, `#Mechanics`, `#UseUnits` | SI units table. | Spec p. 14; Elaboration p. 13 |

## Topic: Statistical sampling
### Topic Summary
Population, sample, sampling methods and critique. The emphasis is on knowing what the data represents and how sampling choices shape conclusions.

### Learning Outcomes Table
| LO ID | Specification learning outcome | Elaboration guidance | Student-friendly meaning | Required depth / boundaries | Suggested lesson tags | Suggested question-bank tags | Visual / widget opportunities | Source pages |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| AS2-SAMP-LO002 | use samples to make informal inferences about the population | No additional elaboration guidance found in the supplied Elaboration Document. | Use samples to make informal inferences about the population | Use the official wording as the ceiling. No extra limits were found in the supplied Elaboration Document, so avoid adding more advanced methods as if they were required. | `#AS2`, `#Statistics`, `#Sampling` | `#AS2`, `#Sampling`, `#CritiqueMethod` | Sample-to-population arrow diagram. | Spec p. 16; Elaboration p. 15 |
| AS2-SAMP-LO003 | demonstrate understanding of and use sampling techniques, including simple random sampling and stratified sampling | No additional elaboration guidance found in the supplied Elaboration Document. | Know how to use sampling techniques, including simple random sampling and stratified sampling | Use the official wording as the ceiling. No extra limits were found in the supplied Elaboration Document, so avoid adding more advanced methods as if they were required. | `#AS2`, `#Statistics`, `#Sampling` | `#AS2`, `#Sampling`, `#CritiqueMethod` | Sampling-method flowchart; stratified table. | Spec p. 16; Elaboration p. 15 |

## Topic: Data presentation and interpretation
### Topic Summary
Reading and critiquing real data, diagrams, averages, spread, correlation, outliers and data cleaning. The large data set should be treated as a living laboratory rather than wallpaper.

# A21: Pure Mathematics
## Unit Overview
**Role in course:** Compulsory A2 pure unit. It assumes AS1 and AS2, extends functions, calculus, trigonometry, series and numerical methods, and carries the heavier synoptic flavour of the full A level.

**Assessment details:** External written examination, 2 hours 30 mins; 150 raw marks if stated; 36% of A level.

**Broad content areas:** Algebra and functions, Co-ordinate geometry in the (x,y) plane, Sequences and series, Trigonometry, Differentiation, Integration, Numerical methods.

**Important student-facing comments:** Keep the official learning outcomes as the boundary. Use elaboration guidance to clarify examples and limits. Treat diagrams, calculators, spreadsheets and graphing tools as planning supports, not as extra syllabus content.

**Links to overarching themes:** mathematical language, modelling, graph/diagram interpretation, calculator/spreadsheet use, communication and evaluation of assumptions.

## Topic: Algebra and functions
### Topic Summary
A2 extends function language: rational expressions, domains and ranges, composites, inverses, modulus, transformations, partial fractions and modelling.

### Learning Outcomes Table

## Topic: Trigonometry
### Topic Summary
A2 trigonometry adds radians, reciprocal and inverse trig functions, compound and double-angle formulae, $a\cos\theta+b\sin\theta$ forms, proofs and contextual problem solving.

## Topic: Differentiation
### Topic Summary
A2 differentiation extends the derivative toolbox to exponential, logarithmic and trigonometric functions, product/quotient/chain rules, implicit and parametric differentiation, and simple differential equations.

## Topic: Integration
### Topic Summary
A2 integration adds standard exponential/log/trig integrals, areas between curves, integration as a limit of sums, substitution, parts, partial fractions, separable differential equations and volumes of revolution.

# A22: Applied Mathematics
## Unit Overview
**Role in course:** Compulsory A2 applied unit. It assumes AS1, AS2 and A21, and is split evenly between mechanics and statistics with modelling, critique and interpretation at centre stage.

**Assessment details:** External written examination, 1 hour 30 mins; 100 raw marks if stated; 24% of A level.

**Broad content areas:** Kinematics, Moments, Impulse and momentum, Probability, Statistical distributions, Statistical hypothesis testing.

**Important student-facing comments:** Keep the official learning outcomes as the boundary. Use elaboration guidance to clarify examples and limits. Treat diagrams, calculators, spreadsheets and graphing tools as planning supports, not as extra syllabus content.

**Links to overarching themes:** mathematical language, modelling, graph/diagram interpretation, calculator/spreadsheet use, communication and evaluation of assumptions.

## Topic: Kinematics
### Topic Summary
A2 applied kinematics uses calculus and vectors for straight-line and two-dimensional motion, including motion under gravity and projectiles.

### Learning Outcomes Table
| LO ID | Specification learning outcome | Elaboration guidance | Student-friendly meaning | Required depth / boundaries | Suggested lesson tags | Suggested question-bank tags | Visual / widget opportunities | Source pages |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

#### A22-KIN-LO001: Calculus in kinematics for motion in a straight line: , ,  and

**Official specification wording:**  
use calculus in kinematics for motion in a straight line: $v=\frac{ds}{dt}$, $a=\frac{dv}{dt}=\frac{d^2s}{dt^2}$, $s=\int v\,dt$ and $v=\int a\,dt$

**Elaboration document guidance:**  
Displacement, velocity and acceleration will be given as functions of time.

**What this means in plain English:**  
Use calculus in kinematics for motion in a straight line: $v=\frac{ds}{dt}$, $a=\frac{dv}{dt}=\frac{d^2s}{dt^2}$, $s=\int v\,dt$ and $v=\int a\,dt$

- communicate the result using correct notation

### Expanded Learning Outcome Notes
#### A22-HT-LO001: The language of statistical hypothesis testing: null hypothesis, alte...

**Official specification wording:**  
demonstrate understanding and use the language of statistical hypothesis testing: null hypothesis, alternative hypothesis, significance level, test statistic, 1-tail test, 2-tail test, critical value, critical region, acceptance region and p-value

**Elaboration document guidance:**  
No additional elaboration guidance found in the supplied Elaboration Document.

**What this means in plain English:**  
Understand and use the language of statistical hypothesis testing: null hypothesis, alternative hypothesis, significance level, test statistic, 1-tail test, 2-tail tes...

**What students should be able to do:**
- identify the relevant events, distribution or test
- calculate probabilities or test values accurately
- interpret the result using correct statistical language

**Required depth and boundaries:**  
Use the official wording as the ceiling. No extra limits were found in the supplied Elaboration Document, so avoid adding more advanced methods as if they were required.

**Useful prerequisites:**  
AS2 binomial distribution, A22 normal distribution, probability, calculator distribution functions, correlation coefficient.

**Typical exam/question-bank tags:**  
`#A22`, `#HypothesisTesting`, `#PValue`, `#CriticalRegion`

**Possible visuals or interactive supports:**

A22-HT-LO002, AS2-PROB-LO001

**Source reference:**  
Specification: p. 24  
Elaboration Document: p. 25-26

#### A22-HT-LO003: A statistical hypothesis test for the proportion in the binomial dist...

**Official specification wording:**  
conduct a statistical hypothesis test for the proportion in the binomial distribution and interpret the results in context

**Elaboration document guidance:**  
Students are not expected to have a formal understanding of Type 1 errors.

**What this means in plain English:**  
Conduct a statistical hypothesis test for the proportion in the binomial distribution and interpret the results in context

**What students should be able to do:**
- translate the context into mathematical variables
- apply the relevant model or method
- interpret and, where needed, critique the answer in context

**Required depth and boundaries:**  
Conduct the test and interpret the outcome. The Elaboration Document says a formal understanding of Type I errors is not expected, even though significance level is interpreted as the chance of incorrectly rejecting $H_0$.

**Useful prerequisites:**  
AS2 binomial distribution, A22 normal distribution, probability, calculator distribution functions, correlation coefficient.

## 9. Suggested Lesson Creation Order
### AS1
1. **Algebra and functions** - Core manipulation and graph skills support almost everything else.
2. **Co-ordinate geometry in the $x,y$ plane** - Uses algebra and introduces geometric modelling with equations.
3. **Sequences and series** - Short topic that reinforces algebraic structure and notation.
4. **Trigonometry** - Needed for later mechanics, calculus examples and A2 trigonometry.
5. **Exponentials and logarithms** - Builds on indices and functions before calculus and modelling.
6. **Differentiation** - Needs algebra, functions and graph interpretation.
7. **Integration** - Best taught after differentiation because it is reverse differentiation at AS.
8. **Vectors** - Useful before AS2 mechanics, especially kinematics and forces.

### AS2
1. **Quantities and units in mechanics** - Gives the mechanics vocabulary and unit discipline.
2. **Kinematics** - Motion ideas come before force explanations.
3. **Forces and Newton's laws** - Uses vectors, trig and kinematics to model physical situations.
4. **Statistical sampling** - Sets up what data represents before analysing it.
5. **Data presentation and interpretation** - Large data set skills and interpretation sit before probability modelling.
6. **Probability** - Needed for binomial distribution.
7. **Statistical distributions** - Uses probability and binomial expansion links.

### A21
1. **Algebra and functions** - A2 function language and partial fractions are needed later.
2. **Co-ordinate geometry in the $(x,y)$ plane** - Parametric forms support differentiation, integration and modelling.
3. **Sequences and series** - Develops proof and approximation before numerical methods.
4. **Trigonometry** - Needed for A2 calculus with trig functions.
5. **Differentiation** - Required before integration techniques and differential equations.
6. **Integration** - Builds on differentiation and partial fractions.
7. **Numerical methods** - Works best after functions, graphs, calculus and approximation are in place.

### A22
1. **Kinematics** - Uses A21 calculus and AS2 motion before other mechanics extensions.
2. **Moments** - Builds on statics and force diagrams.
3. **Impulse and momentum** - Adds before/after mechanics and conservation reasoning.
4. **Probability** - Conditional probability prepares the statistical modelling layer.
5. **Statistical distributions** - Normal distribution supports hypothesis testing.
6. **Statistical hypothesis testing** - Requires distribution knowledge, probability language and interpretation.

---
## 11. Ambiguities, Extraction Issues, and Assumptions
- Page references use the printed page numbers shown inside the PDFs, not zero-based PDF page indices.
- Several mathematical formulae were garbled in raw PDF text extraction. Formulae in this map were normalised using the rendered PDF pages where needed, especially graph transformation formulae, circle equations, logarithm examples, conditional probability, kinematics formulae and normal-distribution notation.
- In AS1 graph transformations, the Specification page was visually inspected and includes $y=f(x)+a$ alongside $y=af(x)$, $y=f(x+a)$ and $y=f(ax)$. The Elaboration Document line lists fewer forms. The Specification was treated as authoritative.
- In A2 1 graph transformations, the Specification page was visually inspected and the transformation list was normalised as $y=af(x)$, $y=f(x)+a$, $y=f(x+a)$ and $y=f(ax)$.
- Where guidance in the Elaboration Document visually aligned with a nearby learning outcome, it was matched to that outcome. Some guidance applies to a cluster rather than a single line, so it is repeated or summarised where useful.
- The document does not add extra mathematical content as official syllabus content. Student-friendly explanations, prerequisites, tags and visual suggestions are planning aids only.
- The word "normal" in A22 LO IDs uses the topic code `NORMAL` because the statistical distributions topic is specifically the normal distribution in A2 2.
